SUNTA introduces a surprise-based chunking method for hierarchical video prediction that uses prediction errors to determine chunk boundaries, and employs a decoupled training strategy to avoid hierarchical collapse. This approach significantly improves long-horizon video prediction, maintaining accuracy over 250 timesteps while baselines degrade within 10 timesteps, which could benefit applications like autonomous driving and robotics. SUNTA uses internal inconsistency as a top-down surprise metric to determine chunk boundaries during imagined rollouts, and its decoupled training strategy preserves surprise signals that would otherwise vanish.

## [G-RRM: Guiding Symbolic Solvers with Neural Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02491) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose G-RRM, a neuro-symbolic approach that integrates Symbol-Equivariant Recurrent Reasoning Models (SE-RRMs) with classical symbolic solvers like backtracking, Glucose 4.1, and CaDiCaL 3.0.0 to improve search efficiency in constraint satisfaction problems. This work demonstrates that neural guidance can significantly accelerate symbolic solvers under specific conditions, potentially improving performance in combinatorial search problems like Sudoku. It provides insights into when and how neuro-symbolic integration yields practical speedups. On 9x9 Sudoku, where SE-RRM solves 91.1% of instances, backtracking achieves a 33.3x median speedup and Glucose 4.1 achieves 1.70x, while CaDiCaL 3.0.0 shows no significant speedup due to its overhead and inability to overwrite hints. The approach requires an expansive search space and a solver architecture that can dynamically overwrite branching choices.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Constraint satisfaction problems (CSPs) involve finding assignments to variables that satisfy given constraints. Symbolic solvers like backtracking and SAT solvers (e.g., Glucose, CaDiCaL) are complete but can be slow on large instances. Neural models can propose solutions quickly but may lack guarantees. G-RRM combines both: neural models generate hints to guide symbolic solvers, aiming for efficiency while preserving correctness.

SPARCLE introduces a speaker-aware grapheme representation model trained with contrastive learning to align graphemes with Wav2Vec2 acoustic representations, replacing traditional G2P systems in TTS. It reduces word error rates by half in extreme low-resource settings compared to standard grapheme-based models. This work addresses the one-to-many mapping problem in grapheme-based TTS by incorporating speaker-specific acoustic variation, significantly improving synthesis quality in low-resource languages or domains. It offers a practical alternative to phoneme-based systems that require G2P conversion, which often fails to capture speaker nuances. SPARCLE uses a contrastive objective conditioned on speaker identity to align grapheme embeddings with Wav2Vec2 acoustic features. The model is designed as a drop-in replacement for G2P modules in existing TTS pipelines, and experiments show it halves word error rates in low-resource settings.

Recent large-scale TTS models have shifted to direct grapheme modeling, but this approach struggles in low-resource settings due to the one-to-many mapping problem. Contrastive learning is a technique that learns to pull similar representations together and push dissimilar ones apart, commonly used for cross-modal alignment.

## [Prompt Compression via Lévy Walk-Guided Graph Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01241) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose RAGP, a method that reformulates prompt compression as redundancy-aware graph pruning on a multiplex graph, using Lévy walks to efficiently identify non-redundant nodes. Experiments on LongBench show RAGP achieves an average score of 49.3 under 4x compression, outperforming existing LLM-based methods like LongLLMLingua (48.8 at 3x compression). This work addresses a key limitation of existing prompt compression methods that treat text as flat token sequences, failing to capture distributed important information. By leveraging graph theory and Lévy walks, RAGP offers a more effective compression technique that can reduce computational costs while preserving critical information for large language models. RAGP constructs a multiplex graph with two layers: fine-grained attention-based dependencies and coarse-grained semantic relations. Lévy walks, which have heavy-tailed step distributions, are used to balance local exploitation and global exploration during node pruning. The code is publicly available at an anonymous repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Prompt compression aims to reduce the length of input prompts to large language models (LLMs) while preserving essential information, thereby lowering computational costs. Existing methods often treat text as flat sequences and fail to capture the distributed nature of important information. A multiplex graph is a graph with multiple types of edges between nodes, allowing joint modeling of different relationships. Lévy walks are random walks with heavy-tailed step distributions, enabling efficient search in heterogeneous structures.

## [Pruned MoE Models: Utility vs. Factual Reliability in Biomedicine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01444) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study systematically evaluates how structured expert pruning affects both utility and factual reliability of Mixture-of-Experts models in the biomedical domain, finding that moderate pruning preserves in-domain utility without immediate reliability decline, but extreme pruning increases hallucination risks. This matters because biomedical NLP applications demand high factual reliability, and prior pruning research focused only on benchmark utility, ignoring reliability. The findings highlight that safe compression depends on task and domain, and that reliability assessment is essential for high-stakes deployment. The study evaluated four MoE models, six pruning methods, and multiple pruning ratios on generation and classification tasks in both in-domain (biomedical) and cross-domain settings. Results showed that when shifting to the general domain, both utility and reliability degrade rapidly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models improve inference efficiency by activating only a subset of experts per input, but they require loading all experts into memory. Structured expert pruning removes entire experts to reduce memory footprint, but prior work mainly evaluated utility on standard benchmarks, not factual reliability. Factual reliability refers to the model's ability to produce accurate, non-hallucinated information, which is critical in high-stakes domains like biomedicine.

This paper introduces the Explainability Stability Index (ESI), a scalar metric measuring SHAP-based explanation drift under adversarial attacks, and evaluates it across multiple classifiers and security datasets. It reveals that gradient-based black-box attacks (ZOO) produce degenerate results on XGBoost, while score-based attacks expose genuine vulnerabilities. This work highlights that prediction robustness and explanation stability are distinct axes requiring joint measurement, which is critical for cybersecurity analysts who rely on SHAP explanations to triage alerts. The two-axis framework (gradient dependence, query efficiency) provides practical guidance for evaluating tree ensemble models. The study evaluates five attacks on Random Forest and XGBoost across four tabular security datasets, finding that ZOO attacks yield apparent robustness index ~0.98 on XGBoost but ESI as low as 0.06-0.16. A step-size ablation explains a counterintuitive PGD anomaly on z-score normalized tabular data.

The Explainability Stability Index (ESI) quantifies attribution drift caused by adversarial perturbations.

## [Rank-Then-Act: Reward-Free Control via Ordinal Progress](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01897) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Rank-Then-Act (RTA), a framework that trains a Vision-Language Model (VLM) as a progress-based ordinal scorer using GRPO, and uses Spearman rank correlation as a reward signal for reinforcement learning without environment rewards. RTA eliminates the need for manually designed reward functions by learning from expert videos, making it scalable to complex tasks. The correlation-based reward signal improves training stability and enables cross-task transfer of a single pretrained scorer. RTA is evaluated on discrete control benchmarks (PyBoy: Catrap, Kirby) and continuous control tasks (PointMaze, MetaWorld), consistently matching or outperforming prior methods. The VLM scorer is trained offline on shuffled frame sequences using GRPO to recover temporal ordering.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning often requires a carefully designed reward function, which is difficult to specify for complex tasks. GRPO is a reinforcement learning algorithm that eliminates the need for a critic model by using group-based advantage estimation. Spearman rank correlation measures the monotonic relationship between two ranked variables, providing a bounded and scale-invariant signal.

## [Population-Based Multi-Objective Training for SSL-GANs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01907) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a population-based evolutionary training strategy for semi-supervised GANs that formulates discriminator learning as a multi-objective optimization problem using Pareto dominance, instead of combining supervised and unsupervised losses into a single scalar. This approach improves training robustness and classification accuracy in semi-supervised GANs, addressing a key instability issue and enabling better utilization of unlabeled data for both classification and generation tasks. The method maintains a population of discriminators ranked by Pareto dominance, exploring different trade-offs between classification accuracy and real/fake discrimination. Experiments on MNIST with limited labels show improved robustness over SSL-GAN and CE-SSL-GAN baselines, with the elitist variant achieving the highest accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Semi-supervised GANs (SSL-GANs) use a discriminator that also acts as a classifier, leveraging unlabeled data to improve classification. However, training is often unstable due to conflicting objectives. Multi-objective optimization using Pareto dominance allows simultaneous optimization of multiple conflicting goals without combining them into a single loss.

## [Low-Latency Task-Oriented Image Transmission with VQ-VAE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01921) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new framework combines VQ-VAE with opportunistic spectrum access to achieve 79x and 3.3x latency reductions for task-oriented image transmission, with only 5.7% and 2.4% drops in classification accuracy compared to conventional coding. This work enables ultra-low-latency communication for real-time AI tasks (e.g., autonomous driving, remote inspection) under limited spectrum and fading channels, bridging task-oriented communication with practical wireless constraints. The framework uses VQ-VAE to compress images into discrete latent codes, transmitted over idle licensed channels via standard digital modulation. A cross-layer latency model accounts for compression, block errors, retransmissions, and stochastic channel access.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional communication systems prioritize reliable data reconstruction, leading to high latency under limited spectrum. Task-oriented communication instead focuses on transmitting only task-relevant information. VQ-VAE learns discrete latent representations, enabling efficient compression. Opportunistic spectrum access allows secondary users to utilize idle licensed spectrum without interfering with primary users.

## [VLM Reliability for Medical Image Quality Under Scrutiny](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01973) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study benchmarks 16 vision-language models (VLMs) on medical image quality assessment under seven corruption types and five severity levels, revealing that pixelation reduces scores by up to 34.4% and that textual attributes like institutional prestige can inflate scores by 17.15%. This work highlights critical reliability gaps in VLMs for medical imaging, where real-world corruptions and biases could undermine diagnostic safety. The findings urge caution in deploying VLMs for automated medical image quality assessment without robust safeguards. The study uses the MediMeta-C dataset across seven modalities (e.g., OCT, mammography) and 16 VLMs. Pixelation caused the largest score drops (mean -20.58%), while brightness had minimal effect (-0.81%). Textual attributes such as equipment age lowered scores by 14.7%, and the largest individual score change was +95.62% for InternVL-8B.

## [Controlled Study Ranks Lightweight CNNs for Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01984) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A controlled study compared nine lightweight CNN architectures across CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and Tiny ImageNet, finding that EfficientNet-B0 offers a strong accuracy-efficiency trade-off, appearing on every Pareto frontier. This study provides practitioners with reliable guidance for selecting lightweight CNNs under resource constraints, highlighting that newer designs offer selective rather than universal gains. EfficientNetV2-S achieved the highest top-1 accuracy on CIFAR-10 (97.57%) and CIFAR-100 (86.98%), while RepViT-M1.0 led Tiny ImageNet (79.87%). EfficientNet-B0 used 79% fewer parameters and 86% fewer GMACs than EfficientNetV2-S while staying within 1.79 percentage points of the best accuracy.

**Background**: Lightweight convolutional neural networks (CNNs) are designed for deployment on resource-constrained devices like mobile phones and edge hardware. This study controls for training recipes and input settings to enable fair comparisons.

## [kNNGuard: Training-Free Guardrail Using LLM Activations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02072) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose kNNGuard, a training-free guardrail that uses hidden activations from an off-the-shelf LLM and multi-layer kNN fusion to classify prompts as safe or unsafe, achieving competitive F1 scores while running 2.7x faster than fine-tuned methods. This work addresses key limitations of existing guardrails—poor generalization and high latency—by offering a training-free, configurable alternative that can be adapted to new domains in seconds, making LLM safety more practical and efficient. kNNGuard requires only 50 labeled prompts and extracts hidden activations from multiple layers of an LLM, fusing activation-space and embedding-space scores via kNN. Domain adaptation involves updating the labeled bank in under 10 seconds, orders of magnitude faster than retraining.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Guardrails are safety mechanisms that detect unsafe, off-topic, or adversarial prompts before they reach an LLM. Traditional guardrails rely on fine-tuning classifiers, which are slow to adapt and computationally expensive. kNNGuard leverages the internal activation space of LLMs—intermediate representations that capture semantic and safety-related features—to perform classification without any training.

## [Evolving WFC Inputs Improves Procedural Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02082) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes evolving the small input examples used by Wave Function Collapse (WFC) rather than evolving complete levels, using WFC as a genotype-to-phenotype mapping. Results show improved generation quality in domains where properties emerge from local relationships, such as maze connectivity maps. This hybrid approach combines the strengths of evolutionary search and WFC, offering a new way to guide procedural content generation when target objectives align with local structure. It has implications for game design and AI-driven content creation, potentially reducing manual effort in generating diverse and high-quality levels. The method was evaluated in two domains: maze connectivity maps and Zelda-style dungeon layouts. While it succeeded in the maze domain, it struggled with Zelda dungeons that require global constraints, highlighting a limitation of the approach.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Wave Function Collapse (WFC) is a constraint-solving algorithm used in procedural generation to produce larger outputs from small example inputs by enforcing local adjacency rules. Evolutionary algorithms optimize solutions through selection and mutation over generations. This paper combines them by evolving the input examples for WFC, treating WFC as a mapping from genotype (input) to phenotype (generated level).

SelectTSL introduces an end-to-end architecture for prompt-guided selective target sound localization, enabling localization of only the user-specified sound source in complex multi-source acoustic scenes. This bridges the gap between sound source localization (SSL) and target sound extraction (TSE), enabling selective localization that preserves spatial information, which is crucial for applications like hearing aids, robotics, and smart environments. The method uses a Prompt-Guided Selective Attention Module (PGSA) to generate prompt-informed embeddings that guide an inter-channel phase difference (IPD) enhancer, jointly estimating direction of arrival (DoA) and target-source cardinality.

## [Bounded Memory Gates Stabilize Quantum Fast-Weight Programmers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02363) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a bounded old-state modulation rule for Self-Modulating Quantum Fast-Weight Programmers (QFWPs) that applies a sign-preserving tanh gate only to the recurrent memory branch, preventing divergence in long-sequence modeling. This stabilization technique enables QFWPs to reliably handle long sequences, which is critical for practical quantum machine learning applications in time-series forecasting and quantum dynamics simulation. The bounded rule leaves the additive update and new-update modulation unchanged, and experiments on CUDA-Q quantum-dynamics tasks and Milan SMS telecommunication data show it removes long-sequence divergence while improving robustness.

**Background**: Quantum Fast-Weight Programmers store temporal information in dynamically programmed variational-circuit parameters instead of recurrent hidden states, offering a quantum approach to sequence modeling. The Self-Modulating variant uses input-dependent gates for updates, but its unbounded old-state multiplier can cause divergence over long sequences.

## [MAGIK: Zero-Shot RL Transfer via Imagination-Based Analogy Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.01623) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose MAGIK, a framework that enables reinforcement learning agents to transfer knowledge to analogous tasks without any interaction with the target environment, using an imagination mechanism to map target entities to source analogues. This work addresses a key limitation of RL—the need for extensive retraining on new tasks—by enabling zero-shot transfer, which could significantly reduce training time and improve generalization in robotics and game AI. MAGIK uses a semi-supervised Variational Autoencoder (VAE) to learn a structured latent space and requires only a small number of human-labeled examples for effective transfer. Experiments on MiniGrid and MuJoCo tasks demonstrate successful zero-shot policy reuse.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning agents typically fail to generalize to new tasks even when they share structural similarities, requiring extensive retraining. Analogical reasoning, a hallmark of human cognition, allows humans to apply prior knowledge to novel situations. MAGIK bridges this gap by combining imagination-based mapping with transfer learning, enabling agents to 'imagine' how a target task maps to a known source task.

This work addresses a fundamental limitation of LLMs in spatial reasoning by enabling principled modality selection, which could significantly improve AI systems' performance in tasks like navigation, layout planning, and diagram understanding. The switching metric combines trustworthiness and complexity signals to estimate when grounding a spatial story into structure is beneficial. The study shows that grid-based representations outperform natural language reasoning by up to 42% across various settings.

Researchers propose a resource-efficient domain-adaptive continuous pre-training (DAP) method using Fully Sharded Data Parallel (FSDP) to specialize LLMs for cybersecurity with only 118.8 million tokens, achieving state-of-the-art results on three benchmarks. This work demonstrates that domain-specific LLMs can be effectively adapted with a fraction of the data previously thought necessary, reducing computational and energy costs by 23-42x, which is crucial for sustainable AI development and practical deployment of specialized cybersecurity assistants. The method adapted three decoder-based architectures (Llama-3.1-8B, DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-14B, Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct) using a curated 126-million-word cybersecurity corpus. The Llama-3.3-70B-Ins-DAP model achieved accuracies of 0.718, 0.933, and 0.864 on CTI-MCQ, CyberMetric, and SecEval benchmarks, surpassing models trained on billions of tokens.

LEFT proposes a unified unsupervised framework for time series anomaly detection that learns to fuse tri-view tokens from time, frequency, and multi-scale views, enforcing time-frequency cycle consistency to capture cross-view inconsistencies. This work addresses a key challenge in unsupervised TSAD where subtle anomalies are invisible in any single view but manifest as cross-view inconsistencies, potentially improving detection accuracy in critical domains like industrial monitoring and healthcare. LEFT introduces adaptive Nyquist-constrained spectral filters to rescale the time series into multiple resolutions, and uses a novel objective that reconstructs fine-grained targets from coarser multi-scale structure, along with a time-frequency cycle consistency constraint.

Traditional methods often rely on a single view (e.g., time domain), but many anomalies are subtle and only detectable as inconsistencies across multiple representations, such as time, frequency, and multi-scale views. LEFT builds on this insight by learning to fuse tokens from these views with explicit cross-view regularization.

This paper establishes data-level conditions that ensure the sensitivity of finite-horizon Pontryagin systems remains uniform as the horizon grows, providing a verifiable certificate for horizon-uniform sensitivity in optimal control computations. This result is significant because it guarantees that finite-horizon optimal control computations do not suffer from deteriorating conditioning as the horizon increases, which could improve the reliability and efficiency of numerical methods in control theory. ## [Hybrid Diffusion Transformer for Audio Editing via Rectified Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20101) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a hybrid two-stage diffusion transformer architecture for instruction-guided audio editing, using rectified flow matching to balance global modeling and efficiency. This approach addresses limitations of existing U-Net-based methods in long-range semantic alignment and instruction understanding, while avoiding the quadratic complexity of full joint-attention transformers, enabling more efficient and accurate audio editing. The architecture performs joint attention at low resolution for coarse alignment, then switches to alternating joint-attention and cross-attention blocks at high resolution for refinement. Experiments show notable gains on tasks with overlapping audio events and complex instructions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative models that learn to denoise data from noise. Rectified flow (or flow matching) is a recent approach that uses a deterministic ODE to interpolate between noise and data, enabling faster sampling. Diffusion transformers (DiT) and MMDiT blocks are transformer-based backbones that offer stronger global modeling than convolutional U-Nets, but joint attention over all tokens incurs quadratic cost.

## [Resonant Brane Splatting for Arbitrary-Scale Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29453) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Resonant Brane Splatting (RBS) is a feed-forward arbitrary-scale super-resolution framework that replaces standard 2D Gaussian primitives with Branes—expressive primitives augmented with internal Gaussian-Hermite modes to model complex textures and edges more efficiently. RBS achieves superior reconstruction quality and speed-quality trade-off compared to prior Gaussian Splatting methods, potentially enabling real-time high-quality image upscaling at arbitrary scales for applications like medical imaging and video processing. Branes assign a distinct color coefficient to each Gaussian-Hermite mode, with the zero-order mode recovering standard Gaussian Splatting and higher-order modes capturing high frequencies. The framework also introduces an efficient differentiable rasterizer with a culling strategy based on the quantum turning point to skip negligible regions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Arbitrary-Scale Super-Resolution (ASR) aims to reconstruct images at continuous magnification factors. Traditional methods use implicit neural decoders which are slow, while recent 2D Gaussian Splatting (GS) methods are faster but struggle with sharp edges and fine textures because Gaussians are smooth low-pass primitives. RBS addresses this by using Branes with internal modes to model local contrast within a single footprint.

## [LLM Framework Translates Natural Language to Strategic Temporal Logic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30441) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose an LLM-based framework that translates natural language descriptions of strategic requirements into ATL/ATL* formulas for multi-agent system verification, and introduce a new expert-validated dataset for this task. This work bridges a critical gap between natural language and formal verification in multi-agent systems, enabling non-expert users to specify strategic properties without deep expertise in temporal logic, potentially accelerating adoption of formal methods in AI safety. Fine-tuned small open-weight models (3-7B parameters) achieve 0.84 semantic accuracy, statistically on par with the best few-shot proprietary baseline (0.86), while keeping requirements on-premises. The study also finds that stronger proprietary models are less reliable as judges, over-rejecting faithful paraphrases.

## [SAEs Detect but Can't Steer Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31699) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper shows that sparse autoencoders (SAEs) can reliably detect and localize semantic concepts in diffusion models, but direct latent intervention causes severe artifacts. The authors propose using SAE activations purely as semantic detectors to identify object regions and replace them with non-object patch embeddings, achieving cleaner erasure than latent steering. This work reveals a fundamental gap between concept detection and intervention in diffusion models, cautioning against using SAE features as control knobs. It provides a safer alternative for unlearning and steering, which is critical for AI safety and interpretability. The method uses SAE activations to identify patch embeddings containing the target object, then replaces them with embeddings from a non-object region. This detection-based replacement preserves the diffusion model's activation statistics, avoiding out-of-distribution artifacts that arise from direct latent steering.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are interpretability tools that learn sparse, monosemantic features from model activations. Prior work assumed these features could be directly manipulated to steer model outputs, but this paper shows that such intervention often pushes activations out of distribution, causing visual artifacts. Diffusion models generate images by iteratively denoising latent representations, and unlearning aims to remove specific concepts (e.g., objects) from the model's output.

Researchers propose BaRA, an LLM-based web agent that combines breadth-first search, liveness verification, and history-based self-reflection to efficiently collect multimodal data from websites under a fixed interaction budget. This addresses a practical gap in LLM-based web agents, which typically focus on task completion but not on efficient, budget-constrained multimodal data collection. Its history-based self-reflection module recovers from execution failures and incomplete outputs.

## [Spectral Geometry Emerges in Quantum Learning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00063) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper shows that training graph-regularized quantum networks reorganizes the output similarity graph, increasing the effective spectral dimension by +0.23, and that edge-resolved two-boson interference can directly probe this restructuring. This work establishes a unified spectral-geometric framework for diagnosing quantum learning systems, potentially enabling new methods for understanding and improving quantum machine learning models. The bosonic enhancement ΔP_uv correlates with the Fiedler edge split |Δv_2| (r = -0.50), linking learned spectral partitions to interference signatures; hardware experiments confirm the predicted interference behavior within shot-noise uncertainty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Spectral geometry studies the relationship between the eigenvalues of operators (like the Laplacian) and the geometric properties of a space. In quantum learning, graph regularization uses a graph structure to enforce smoothness of the learned function. The Fiedler value (second smallest eigenvalue of the Laplacian) is a measure of graph connectivity.

## [Let AI Models Use Their Own Judgement](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Jul/3/judgement/#atom-everything) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Simon Willison shares a tip from a fireside chat with the Claude Code team: instead of dictating how AI models like Fable should work, let them use their own judgement for tasks like testing and model selection. He also implemented a prompt that tells Claude Code to delegate coding tasks to lower-power models via subagents, which has reduced token consumption. This approach can significantly reduce token usage and costs when using expensive top-tier models like Fable, making AI-assisted development more efficient and affordable. It also highlights a shift in prompt engineering from micromanaging AI to trusting its judgement, which could improve developer productivity. The tip was shared by Cat Wu and Thariq Shihipar from the Claude Code team at AIE. Simon's prompt "For all coding tasks use your judgement to decide an appropriate lower power model and run that in a subagent" was saved as a memory file by Claude Code, specifying when to use Sonnet or Haiku models.

**Background**: Claude Code is an agentic coding system from Anthropic that reads codebases, edits files, and runs commands. Token optimization techniques like prompt compression and model delegation help reduce expenses when using large language models.

Ablation studies confirmed the necessity of each training stage for balancing domain expertise and general ability.

## [LLMs as Teaching Assistants for Math Exam Grading](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01247) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study evaluates six LLM configurations (including Gemini 3.1 Pro Extended, ChatGPT 5.5 Thinking, and Claude Opus 4.7) for grading open-ended undergraduate discrete mathematics exams, comparing strict (BASELINE) and liberal (LIBERAL) rubric-following policies. This research explores the practical usability of LLMs in education, potentially reducing grading workload for instructors while maintaining consistency. The findings highlight that liberal partial-credit prompting reduces grading errors across all models, suggesting a path toward reliable AI-assisted grading. ChatGPT 5.5 Thinking (LIBERAL) achieved the lowest average question-level MAE (1.87) and RMSE (2.53), while Gemini 3.1 Pro Extended (LIBERAL) had the lowest total-score MAE (8.00) and RMSE (10.66). However, the strongest total-score Pearson correlation (0.58) was under Gemini 3.1 Pro Extended (BASELINE), indicating a trade-off between calibration and rank preservation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Open-ended mathematics exams assess reasoning and proof construction but are difficult to grade consistently at scale. LLMs have been explored for automated grading, but their reliability in applying partial-credit rubrics remains a challenge. This study uses six contemporary LLMs and two grading policies to evaluate their agreement with human graders.

## [Indian Dermatologists Surveyed on AI Use and AD Workflow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01252) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A nationwide survey of 377 Indian dermatologists found that 49.9% currently use AI, primarily general large language models for administrative and academic tasks rather than specialized image analysis. The survey, commissioned by the Society for Eczema Studies, focused on clinical challenges in atopic dermatitis (AD) workflow management. This study highlights a gap between AI deployment and clinical needs in dermatology, suggesting that clinician-supervised workflow tools may be more valuable than standalone diagnostic AI. The findings can guide future AI development for chronic disease management, particularly in resource-limited settings. Patient adherence (61.3%) and treatment planning for refractory cases (57.0%) were reported as top challenges, while diagnostic uncertainty was lower (48.0%). In AD care, severity scoring was a challenge for 47.7% and had the lowest satisfaction among workflow areas. AI users were more likely to worry about patient self-misdiagnosis and anxiety.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Atopic dermatitis (AD) is a chronic inflammatory skin condition affecting millions worldwide. Severity scoring tools like SCORAD and EASI are essential for assessing disease burden and treatment efficacy, but they can be time-consuming and subjective. AI in dermatology has largely focused on image-based diagnosis, while workflow management for chronic diseases like AD has received less attention.

## [Multi-modal AI Pipeline for Railway Crossing Safety](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01365) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a proof-of-concept multi-modal AI pipeline that combines images and structured accident data to assess railway crossing safety, achieving a macro F1 score of 0.757 for risk classification and an RMSE of 0.078 for FRA-based safety scores. This work demonstrates the potential of multi-modal AI for infrastructure safety assessment, which could help prioritize safety improvements at railway crossings and reduce accidents. It also aligns with regulatory frameworks like the FRA's safety scoring system. The pipeline uses a routed fine-tuned compact VLM (Vision-Language Model) and incorporates both visual cues from images and structured data from official accident reports. The system identifies HIGH-RISK and LOW-RISK crossings with a macro F1 score of 0.757 and estimates FRA-based safety scores with a correlation of 0.492.

A new study evaluates cluster-based semantic chunking against fixed-size and recursive chunking for RAG on academic theses, finding no performance improvement and revealing that the RAGAs faithfulness metric has limited reliability in this context. This challenges the assumption that more sophisticated chunking strategies automatically improve RAG quality, and highlights the need for better evaluation metrics, which is crucial for practitioners building RAG systems on long documents. ## [TUDUM: Turkish-Thinking Pipeline for Qwen3.5-27B](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01927) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced TUDUM, a pipeline that adapts the Qwen3.5-27B thinking model to reason in Turkish by applying supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on 15,991 Turkish examples and then GRPO-based reinforcement learning on Turkish math problems. This work addresses the challenge of making large language models reason in a language other than English, which is important for expanding AI accessibility to non-English speakers. The mixed results highlight the difficulty of preserving benchmark performance while enforcing language-specific reasoning. SFT reduced response length and thinking exhaustion but lowered benchmark accuracy, while GRPO recovered some math performance (e.g., AIME24) but did not exceed the base model on the Macro-6 average. The released step-50 model is publicly available.

Researchers propose RadiomicNet, a two-stream hybrid architecture that integrates handcrafted radiomics features (GLCM, LBP) into a lightweight MobileNetV2-based encoder-decoder via a novel Radiomics Attention Gate (RAG) and a Radiomics Consistency Loss, achieving improved calibration error and competitive segmentation performance on BUSI and Kvasir-SEG datasets. This work addresses the lack of interpretability in deep learning medical image segmentation by providing ante-hoc explanations grounded in clinically meaningful texture features, while maintaining high performance with significantly fewer parameters, potentially enabling deployment in resource-constrained clinical settings. RadiomicNet achieves a Dice Similarity Coefficient of 0.763 on BUSI and 0.854 on Kvasir-SEG, outperforming U-KAN by 1.2% and 1.8% respectively, with only 3.27M parameters (9.5x fewer than U-Net, 4.3x fewer than U-KAN). The Expected Calibration Error (ECE) is reduced from 0.142 to 0.118.

**Background**: Medical image segmentation is crucial for diagnosis but deep learning models often lack interpretability and require large parameters. Radiomics extracts quantitative texture features (e.g., GLCM, LBP) from images, which are clinically interpretable. RadiomicNet combines these handcrafted features with deep learning to provide ante-hoc interpretability without post-hoc approximations.

## [GAP-GDRNet: Attention-Enhanced Pose Estimation for Spacecraft](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02360) ⭐️ 6.0/10

GAP-GDRNet introduces attention-based feature refinement (AFR) and patch-level geometric self-attention (PGSA) modules into the GDR-Net pipeline to improve monocular 6D pose estimation for spacecraft. This work addresses critical challenges in non-cooperative spacecraft rendezvous and on-orbit servicing, where weak texture, occlusion, and illumination changes make pose estimation difficult. Improved monocular pose sensing can enhance autonomous space operations without requiring depth sensors. The AFR module reinforces global spacecraft structure and local weak-texture cues before dense geometric prediction, while PGSA relates downsampled geometric patches within Patch-PnP for final pose regression. The method uses a Blender-based annotation pipeline to generate training data with target masks, visible-region masks, dense model-coordinate maps, camera intrinsics, and 6D pose labels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

**Background**: Monocular 6D object pose estimation aims to determine the 3D rotation and translation of an object from a single RGB image. GDR-Net is a geometry-guided direct regression network that achieves real-time pose estimation by predicting dense geometric correspondences and using a Patch-PnP module to regress the pose. However, spacecraft images often suffer from weak surface texture, thin appendages, and partial occlusion, which degrade performance of standard methods.

## [Policy-Based RL for 20 Questions Game](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.07645) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A policy-based reinforcement learning method is proposed for the 20 Questions game, where the questioner agent learns optimal question selection through interactions with users, using a reward network to estimate more informative rewards. This work demonstrates the application of policy-based RL to a classic game, showing robustness to noisy answers and independence from a knowledge base, which could inspire similar approaches in interactive question-answering systems. The method outperforms an entropy-based engineering system and achieves competitive performance in noise-free simulations; it uses a reward network to address the challenge of sparse and delayed rewards in the game.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 3, 04:00

Policy-based RL methods directly learn the policy (mapping from states to actions) without requiring a value function, which can be advantageous for handling continuous or stochastic action spaces. The 20 Questions game is a classic deductive reasoning game where the questioner must guess a hidden object by asking up to 20 yes/no questions.

## [SEPS: Semantic-Enhanced Patch Slimming for Cross-Modal Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01390) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose the Semantic-Enhanced Patch Slimming (SEPS) framework, which uses a dual-granularity semantic calibration mechanism to integrate dense MLLM outputs with sparse captions, reducing patch redundancy and ambiguity in fine-grained cross-modal alignment. This work improves fine-grained image-text retrieval by up to 86% in rSum across diverse models, addressing a key bottleneck in multimodal applications like visual question answering. SEPS employs a two-stage mechanism: first integrating unified semantics from dense and sparse texts to identify salient patches, then using relevance-aware selection with mean value computation to highlight crucial patch-word correspondences. Experiments on Flickr30K and MS-COCO show superior performance.

**Background**: Fine-grained cross-modal alignment aims to match local visual regions with textual tokens, but suffers from patch redundancy (many patches are irrelevant) and ambiguity (patches may correspond to multiple concepts). Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) can generate dense descriptions, but may conflict with original sparse captions. SEPS builds on prior work like LAPS by incorporating semantic enhancement from MLLMs.
